28: func (c *CLab) Destroy(ctx context.Context, options ...DestroyOption) (err error) {
29: 	opts := NewDestroyOptions()
30: 
31: 	for _, opt := range options {
32: 		opt(opts)
33: 	}
34: 
35: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
36: 		return c.destroyWithLabRuntime(ctx, opts)
37: 	}
38: 
39: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
40: 
41: 	switch {
42: 	case opts.all:
43: 		containers, err = c.ListContainers(ctx)
44: 	case c.TopoPaths.TopologyFilenameAbsPath() != "":
45: 		containers, err = c.ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(ctx)
46: 		if err == nil {
47: 			var tailscaleContainers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
48: 			tailscaleContainers, err = c.discoverTailscaleContainers(ctx, opts.nodeFilter)
49: 			containers = append(containers, tailscaleContainers...)
50: 		}
51: 	default:
52: 		var listOpts []ListOption
53: 
54: 		if c.Config.Name != "" {
55: 			listOpts = []ListOption{WithListLabName(c.Config.Name)}
56: 		} else {
57: 			listOpts = []ListOption{WithListclabLabelExists()}
58: 		}
59: 
60: 		containers, err = c.ListContainers(ctx, listOpts...)
61: 	}
62: 
63: 	if err != nil {
64: 		return err
65: 	}
66: 
67: 	// topo will hold the reference to the topology file
68: 	// as the key and the respective lab directory as the referenced value
69: 	topos := map[string]string{}
70: 
71: 	for idx := range containers {
72: 		topoFile, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.TopoFile]
73: 		if !ok {
74: 			continue
75: 		}
76: 
77: 		topos[topoFile] = filepath.Dir(containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeLabDir])
78: 	}
79: 
80: 	// failedLabDirs records the lab directories whose destroy failed or was never
81: 	// attempted. --cleanup keeps those directories: deleting the lab directory of
82: 	// a lab that is still running would discard the state file destroy needs for
83: 	// its management network cleanup on the next attempt.
84: 	failedLabDirs := map[string]bool{}
85: 
86: 	defer func() {
87: 		if opts.cleanup {
88: 			if cleanupErr := c.destroyLabDirs(topos, opts.all, failedLabDirs); cleanupErr != nil {
89: 				err = errors.Join(err, cleanupErr)
90: 			}
91: 		}
92: 	}()
93: 
94: 	if len(topos) == 0 {
95: 		return nil
96: 	}
97: 
98: 	log.Debugf("got the following topologies for destroy: %+v", topos)
99: 
100: 	// if all, and cli doesn't have --yes flag, and in a terminal -- prompt user confirmation
101: 	if opts.all && opts.terminalPrompt && term.IsTerminal(int(os.Stdin.Fd())) {
102: 		err := cliPromptToDestroyAll(topos)
103: 		if err != nil {
104: 			return err
105: 		}
106: 	}
107: 
108: 	var errs []error
109: 
110: 	for topo, labDir := range topos {
111: 		cc, err := c.makeCopyForDestroy(ctx, topo, labDir, *opts)
112: 		if err != nil {
113: 			log.Errorf("error creating clab instance for topo %q: %v", topo, err)
114: 
115: 			errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf(
116: 				"error creating clab instance for topo %q: %w", topo, err))
117: 			failedLabDirs[labDir] = true
118: 
119: 			continue
120: 		}
121: 
122: 		err = cc.destroy(ctx, opts.maxWorkers, opts.keepMgmtNet)
123: 		if err != nil {
124: 			log.Errorf("Error occurred during the %s lab deletion: %v", cc.Config.Name, err)
125: 			errs = append(errs, err)
126: 			failedLabDirs[labDir] = true
127: 		}
128: 	}
129: 
130: 	if len(errs) != 0 {
131: 		return fmt.Errorf("error(s) occurred during the deletion. Check log messages")
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	return nil
135: }
136: 
137: // creates a mostly cloned version of the current c but set to the new topology, and with the
138: // necessary steps (mgmt network things) handled preparing the new CLab for destruction.
139: func (c *CLab) makeCopyForDestroy(
140: 	ctx context.Context,
141: 	topo,
142: 	labDir string,
143: 	opts DestroyOptions,
144: ) (*CLab, error) {
145: 	newOpts := []ClabOption{
146: 		WithTimeout(c.timeout),
147: 		// a topology edited after deploy may no longer pass the mgmt-net link checks,
148: 		// which must not prevent the removal of the deployed lab.
149: 		WithSkippedMgmtNetLinksCheck(),
150: 	}
151: 
152: 	// Try to load topology file if it exists, otherwise use lab name only.
153: 	// This allows destroying labs even when the topology file has been deleted.
154: 	// WithTopoPath / WithLabNameOnly must run before WithNodeFilter so filterClabNodes
155: 	// sees topology nodes (see filterClabNodes in clab.go).
156: 	varsFiles := opts.varsFiles
157: 	if len(varsFiles) == 0 {
158: 		varsFiles = c.TopoPaths.VarsFilenamesAbsPath()
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	if clabutils.FileOrDirExists(topo) {
162: 		newOpts = append(newOpts, WithTopoPath(topo, varsFiles))
163: 	} else {
164: 		// Derive lab name from lab directory (format: clab-<labname>)
165: 		labName := filepath.Base(labDir)
166: 		labName = strings.TrimPrefix(labName, "clab-")
167: 		log.Debugf("topology file %q not found, using lab name %q for destroy", topo, labName)
168: 		newOpts = append(newOpts, WithLabNameOnly(labName))
169: 	}
170: 
171: 	newOpts = append(newOpts,
172: 		WithNodeFilter(opts.nodeFilter),
173: 		// during destroy we don't want to check bind paths
174: 		// as it is irrelevant for this command.
175: 		WithSkippedBindsPathsCheck(),
176: 		WithRuntime(
177: 			c.globalRuntimeName,
178: 			&clabruntime.RuntimeConfig{
179: 				Debug:            c.Config.Debug,
180: 				Timeout:          c.timeout,
181: 				GracefulShutdown: opts.graceful,
182: 			},
183: 		),
184: 	)
185: 
186: 	if opts.keepMgmtNet {
187: 		newOpts = append(newOpts, WithKeepMgmtNet())
188: 	}
189: 
190: 	cc, err := NewContainerLab(newOpts...)
191: 	if err != nil {
192: 		return nil, err
193: 	}
194: 	if labDir != "" && clabutils.FileOrDirExists(labDir) {
195: 		// adjust the labdir. Usually we take the PWD. but now on destroy time,
196: 		// we might be in a different Dir.
197: 		err = cc.TopoPaths.SetLabDir(labDir)
198: 		if err != nil {
199: 			return nil, err
200: 		}
201: 	}
202: 
203: 	// populate the management network state (bridge names) destroy needs for the
204: 	// removal of the iptables rules, without creating networks that are missing:
205: 	// a lab deployed with a management network override leaves the topology's
206: 	// default network uncreated, and creating it here would collide with the
207: 	// subnets the override is using.
208: 	if !cc.mgmtNetworksSkipped() {
209: 		var bridged clabtypes.MgmtNetworks
210: 		for _, m := range cc.allMgmtNetworks() {
211: 			if m.Driver != clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
212: 				bridged = append(bridged, m)
213: 			}
214: 		}
215: 		if err = cc.resolveExistingMgmtNetworks(ctx, bridged); err != nil {
216: 			return nil, err
217: 		}
218: 	}
219: 
220: 	err = cc.setMgmtNetLinkBridges()
221: 	if err != nil {
222: 		return nil, err
223: 	}
224: 
225: 	err = cc.ResolveLinks()
226: 	if err != nil {
227: 		return nil, err
228: 	}
229: 
230: 	return cc, nil
231: }
232: 
233: // resolveExistingMgmtNetworks inspects the management networks that already exist
234: // so their bridge names are populated, e.g. for the iptables cleanup on destroy.
235: // Networks that do not exist are skipped: they hold no bridge yet, and the callers
236: // must not create management networks. Runtimes that cannot check network existence
237: // keep the create-or-inspect behavior.
238: func (c *CLab) resolveExistingMgmtNetworks(
239: 	ctx context.Context,
240: 	networks clabtypes.MgmtNetworks,
241: ) error {
242: 	var existing clabtypes.MgmtNetworks
243: 
244: 	for _, m := range networks {
245: 		rt, rtErr := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
246: 		if rtErr != nil {
247: 			// runtimes without multiple network support never created extra networks
248: 			log.Debug(
249: 				"Skipping management network resolution", "network", m.Network, "error", rtErr,
250: 			)
251: 			continue
252: 		}
253: 
254: 		checker, ok := rt.(clabruntime.MgmtNetworkChecker)
255: 		if !ok {
256: 			existing = append(existing, m)
257: 			continue
258: 		}
259: 
260: 		exists, err := checker.MgmtNetworkExists(ctx)
261: 		if err != nil {
262: 			return err
263: 		}
264: 		if !exists {
265: 			log.Debug("Skipping management network that does not exist", "network", m.Network)
266: 			continue
267: 		}
268: 
269: 		existing = append(existing, m)
270: 	}
271: 
272: 	return c.createNetworks(ctx, existing)
273: }
274: 
275: // destroyLabDirs removes the lab directories of the destroyed labs. Directories
276: // recorded in failedLabDirs are kept: their labs are still running, and the
277: // directory holds the lab state a later destroy attempt relies on.
278: func (c *CLab) destroyLabDirs(
279: 	topos map[string]string,
280: 	all bool,
281: 	failedLabDirs map[string]bool,
282: ) error {
283: 	if len(topos) == 0 {
284: 		log.Info("no containerlab containers found")
285: 
286: 		if !all {
287: 			labDir := c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()
288: 			if labDir == "" {
289: 				// Try to infer the precise lab directory when TopologyLabDir is not set,
290: 				// e.g. destroy --name without topology file. Only look for an exact
291: 				// clab-<name> match, never a wildcard.
292: 				if c.Config != nil && c.Config.Name != "" {
293: 					candidates := []string{}
294: 					if base := os.Getenv("CLAB_LABDIR_BASE"); base != "" {
295: 						candidates = append(candidates, filepath.Join(base, "clab-"+c.Config.Name))
296: 					}
297: 					if c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileIsSet() {
298: 						candidates = append(
299: 							candidates,
300: 							filepath.Join(c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir(), "clab-"+c.Config.Name),
301: 						)
302: 					} else {
303: 						if wd, err := os.Getwd(); err == nil && wd != "" {
304: 							candidates = append(
305: 								candidates,
306: 								filepath.Join(wd, "clab-"+c.Config.Name),
307: 							)
308: 						}
309: 					}
310: 					for _, cand := range candidates {
311: 						if _, err := os.Stat(cand); err == nil {
312: 							labDir = cand
313: 							break
314: 						}
315: 					}
316: 					if labDir == "" {
317: 						log.Info(
318: 							"lab directory not found, nothing to clean",
319: 							"candidates",
320: 							candidates,
321: 						)
322: 						return nil
323: 					}
324: 				} else {
325: 					log.Info("lab directory not found, nothing to clean")
326: 					return nil
327: 				}
328: 			}
329: 
330: 			// Safety: only remove directories that look like a lab directory.
331: 			base := filepath.Base(labDir)
332: 			if !strings.HasPrefix(base, "clab-") {
333: 				log.Warnf("refusing to remove lab directory %q: unexpected name", labDir)
334: 				return nil
335: 			}
336: 			if c.Config != nil && c.Config.Name != "" {
337: 				expected := "clab-" + c.Config.Name
338: 				if base != expected {
339: 					log.Warnf(
340: 						"refusing to remove lab directory %q: does not match expected %q",
341: 						labDir,
342: 						expected,
343: 					)
344: 					return nil
345: 				}
346: 			}
347: 
348: 			if _, err := os.Stat(labDir); os.IsNotExist(err) {
349: 				log.Info("lab directory not found, nothing to clean", "path", labDir)
350: 				return nil
351: 			} else if err != nil {
352: 				log.Debug("failed to stat lab directory, skipping", "path", labDir, "error", err)
353: 				return nil
354: 			}
355: 
356: 			log.Info("Removing lab directory", "path", labDir)
357: 			if err := os.RemoveAll(labDir); err != nil {
358: 				log.Errorf("error deleting lab directory: %v", err)
359: 				return err
360: 			}
361: 		}
362: 
363: 		return nil
364: 	}
365: 
366: 	for _, labdir := range topos {
367: 		if failedLabDirs[labdir] {
368: 			log.Warnf(
369: 				"keeping lab directory %s: the lab was not destroyed, so its state is kept for a later attempt",
370: 				labdir,
371: 			)
372: 
373: 			continue
374: 		}
375: 
376: 		err := os.RemoveAll(labdir)
377: 		if err != nil {
378: 			log.Errorf("error deleting lab directory: %v", err)
379: 		}
380: 	}
381: 
382: 	return nil
383: }
384: 
385: func (c *CLab) destroy(ctx context.Context, maxWorkers uint, keepMgmtNet bool) error {
386: 	var containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
387: 	var orphanSidecars []clabruntime.GenericContainer
388: 	var err error
389: 
390: 	// If we have nodes defined (from topology file), list containers by node.
391: 	// Otherwise, list containers by lab name label (for destroy-by-name-only case).
392: 	if len(c.Nodes) > 0 {
393: 		containers, err = c.ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(ctx)
394: 	} else if c.Config.Name != "" {
395: 		containers, err = c.ListContainers(ctx, WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))
396: 	}
397: 
398: 	if err != nil {
399: 		return err
400: 	}
401: 
402: 	if len(c.Nodes) == 0 {
403: 		c.addMgmtNetworksFromContainers(containers)
404: 	}
405: 
406: 	if len(c.Nodes) > 0 {
407: 		tailscaleContainers, err := c.discoverTailscaleContainers(ctx, c.nodeFilter)
408: 		if err != nil {
409: 			return err
410: 		}
411: 		for _, ctr := range tailscaleContainers {
412: 			if _, desired := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]]; desired {
413: 				continue
414: 			}
415: 			containers = append(containers, ctr)
416: 			if ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] == "" {
417: 				orphanSidecars = append(orphanSidecars, ctr)
418: 			}
419: 		}
420: 	}
421: 
422: 	if len(containers) == 0 {
423: 		if len(c.Nodes) > 0 && len(c.Links) > 0 {
424: 			c.deleteSpecialLinkNodes(ctx)
425: 		}
426: 		return nil
427: 	}
428: 
429: 	if maxWorkers == 0 {
430: 		maxWorkers = uint(len(containers))
431: 	}
432: 
433: 	log.Info("Destroying lab", "name", c.Config.Name)
434: 
435: 	// If we have nodes defined, use the normal node-based deletion.
436: 	// Otherwise, delete containers directly via the runtime (for destroy-by-name-only case).
437: 	if len(c.Nodes) > 0 {
438: 		c.preDestroyNodes(ctx, slices.Collect(maps.Values(c.Nodes)), maxWorkers)
439: 
440: 		err := clablinks.CleanupFilteredLinks(
441: 			ctx,
442: 			c.Config.Topology.Links,
443: 			c.Config.Name,
444: 			c.nodeFilter,
445: 		)
446: 		if err != nil {
447: 			return err
448: 		}
449: 		c.deleteNodes(ctx, maxWorkers)
450: 		c.deleteContainersDirect(ctx, orphanSidecars)
451: 	} else {
452: 		c.deleteContainersDirect(ctx, containers)
453: 	}
454: 
455: 	c.deleteToolContainers(ctx)
456: 
457: 	log.Info("Removing host entries", "path", "/etc/hosts")
458: 
459: 	err = c.DeleteEntriesFromHostsFile()
460: 	if err != nil {
461: 		return fmt.Errorf("error while trying to clean up the hosts file: %w", err)
462: 	}
463: 
464: 	log.Info("Removing SSH config", "path", c.TopoPaths.SSHConfigPath())
465: 
466: 	err = c.RemoveSSHConfig(c.TopoPaths)
467: 	if err != nil {
468: 		log.Errorf("failed to remove ssh config file: %v", err)
469: 	}
470: 
471: 	// delete container network namespaces symlinks
472: 	for _, node := range c.Nodes {
473: 		err = node.DeleteNetnsSymlink()
474: 		if err != nil {
475: 			return fmt.Errorf("error while deleting netns symlinks: %w", err)
476: 		}
477: 	}
478: 
479: 	// delete every defined management network, including extra networks
480: 	// that apply may have left without nodes, and networks a previous
481: 	// deployment recorded in the lab state but the topology no longer defines
482: 	for _, m := range c.mgmtNetworksForCleanup() {
483: 		if m.Network == "bridge" || keepMgmtNet {
484: 			continue
485: 		}
486: 		log.Debugf("Calling DeleteNet method. Management network value is: %+v", m)
487: 
488: 		rt, rtErr := c.mgmtRuntime(c.globalRuntimeName, m)
489: 		if rtErr != nil {
490: 			// runtimes without multiple network support never created extra networks
491: 			log.Debug("Skipping management network deletion", "network", m.Network, "error", rtErr)
492: 			continue
493: 		}
494: 		if err = rt.DeleteNet(ctx); err != nil {
495: 			switch {
496: 			case err.Error() == fmt.Sprintf("Error: No such network: %s", m.Network):
497: 			case strings.Contains(err.Error(), fmt.Sprintf(" network %s not found", m.Network)):
498: 			default:
499: 				log.Error(err)
500: 			}
501: 		}
502: 	}
503: 
504: 	return nil
505: }
506: 
507: func (c *CLab) addMgmtNetworksFromContainers(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer) {
508: 	for _, ctr := range containers {
509: 		switch name := ctr.NetworkName; name {
510: 		case "", "unknown", "bridge", "host", "none":
511: 		default:
512: 			if c.mgmtNetByNetwork(name).Network == name {
513: 				continue
514: 			}
515: 			c.Config.MgmtNetworks = append(c.Config.MgmtNetworks, &clabtypes.MgmtNet{
516: 				Network:        name,
517: 				Bridge:         ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeMgmtNetBr],
518: 				ExternalAccess: new(true),
519: 			})
520: 		}
521: 	}
522: }
523: 
524: // mgmtNetworksForCleanup returns the management networks to delete on destroy:
525: // the topology-defined ones plus any network a previous deployment recorded in
526: // the lab state but that the current topology no longer defines. Without the
527: // state record a network dropped from the mgmt list would never be removed.
528: func (c *CLab) mgmtNetworksForCleanup() clabtypes.MgmtNetworks {
529: 	networks := c.allMgmtNetworks()
530: 
531: 	state, err := c.LoadState()
532: 	if err != nil {
533: 		log.Warn("Unable to load the management networks recorded in the lab state", "error", err)
534: 		return networks
535: 	}
536: 	if state == nil {
537: 		return networks
538: 	}
539: 
540: 	defined := make(map[string]bool, len(networks))
541: 	for _, m := range networks {
542: 		defined[m.Network] = true
543: 	}
544: 	for _, name := range state.MgmtNetworks {
545: 		if defined[name] {
546: 			continue
547: 		}
548: 		defined[name] = true
549: 		// ExternalAccess mirrors the default every management network gets, so
550: 		// the forwarding rule installed for the network is cleaned up too.
551: 		networks = append(networks, &clabtypes.MgmtNet{
552: 			Network:        name,
553: 			ExternalAccess: new(true),
554: 		})
555: 	}
556: 
557: 	return networks
558: }
559: 
560: func (c *CLab) deleteApplyNodes(ctx context.Context, plan *applyPlan) error {
561: 	nodeNames := make([]string, 0, len(plan.deletedNodeSet)+len(plan.recreatedNodeSet))
562: 	for nodeName := range plan.deletedNodeSet {
563: 		nodeNames = append(nodeNames, nodeName)
564: 	}
565: 	for nodeName := range plan.recreatedNodeSet {
566: 		nodeNames = append(nodeNames, nodeName)
567: 	}
568: 	sort.Strings(nodeNames)
569: 
570: 	hookNodes := make([]clabnodes.Node, 0, len(nodeNames))
571: 	var tailscaleContainers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
572: 	for _, nodeName := range nodeNames {
573: 		runtimeNode := plan.currentNodes[nodeName]
574: 		if runtimeNode == nil {
575: 			return fmt.Errorf("runtime node %q not found", nodeName)
576: 		}
577: 		if runtimeNode.external || runtimeNode.rootNamespaceBased {
578: 			continue
579: 		}
580: 		if len(runtimeNode.containers) > 0 &&
581: 			runtimeNode.containers[0].Labels[clabconstants.NodeKind] == clabnodestailscale.KindName {
582: 			tailscaleContainers = append(tailscaleContainers, runtimeNode.containers...)
583: 			continue
584: 		}
585: 		if node, ok := c.Nodes[nodeName]; ok {
586: 			hookNodes = append(hookNodes, node)
587: 		}
588: 	}
589: 	var logoutWg sync.WaitGroup
590: 	for _, ctr := range tailscaleContainers {
591: 		logoutWg.Go(func() { c.logoutTailscaleContainer(ctx, ctr) })
592: 	}
593: 	c.preDestroyNodes(ctx, hookNodes, 0)
594: 	logoutWg.Wait()
595: 
596: 	for _, nodeName := range nodeNames {
597: 		runtimeNode := plan.currentNodes[nodeName]
598: 		if runtimeNode.external || runtimeNode.rootNamespaceBased {
599: 			continue
600: 		}
601: 
602: 		for i := len(runtimeNode.containers) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
603: 			ctr := runtimeNode.containers[i]
604: 			if len(ctr.Names) == 0 {
605: 				return fmt.Errorf("runtime container for node %q has no name", nodeName)
606: 			}
607: 
608: 			runtime := ctr.Runtime
609: 			if runtime == nil {
610: 				runtime = c.globalRuntime()
611: 			}
612: 
613: 			action := "Deleting node"
614: 			if _, recreate := plan.recreatedNodeSet[nodeName]; recreate {
615: 				action = "Deleting node for recreate"
616: 			}
617: 			log.Info(action, "node", nodeName, "container", ctr.Names[0])
618: 			if err := runtime.DeleteContainer(ctx, ctr.Names[0]); err != nil {
619: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed deleting node %q: %w", nodeName, err)
620: 			}
621: 
622: 			_ = clabutils.DeleteNetnsSymlink(ctr.Names[0])
623: 		}
624: 	}
625: 
626: 	return nil
627: }
628: 
629: // preDestroyNodes runs the PreDestroy stage of the given nodes concurrently, before any of them
630: // is deleted. Nodes without a container are skipped. A workers value of 0 runs all hooks at once.
631: func (*CLab) preDestroyNodes(ctx context.Context, nodes []clabnodes.Node, workers uint) {
632: 	if workers == 0 || workers > uint(len(nodes)) {
633: 		workers = uint(len(nodes))
634: 	}
635: 
636: 	sem := make(chan struct{}, workers)
637: 	wg := new(sync.WaitGroup)
638: 
639: 	for _, n := range nodes {
640: 		wg.Add(1)
641: 		sem <- struct{}{}
642: 
643: 		go func() {
644: 			defer wg.Done()
645: 			defer func() { <-sem }()
646: 
647: 			if n.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.NotFound {
648: 				return
649: 			}
650: 
651: 			if err := n.PreDestroy(ctx); err != nil {
652: 				log.Warnf("node %q pre-destroy hook failed: %v", n.Config().ShortName, err)
653: 			}
654: 		}()
655: 	}
656: 
657: 	wg.Wait()
658: }
659: 
660: func (c *CLab) deleteNodes(ctx context.Context, workers uint) {
661: 	wg := new(sync.WaitGroup)
662: 
663: 	concurrentChan := make(chan clabnodes.Node)
664: 
665: 	workerFunc := func(i uint, input chan clabnodes.Node, wg *sync.WaitGroup) {
666: 		defer wg.Done()
667: 
668: 		for {
669: 			select {
670: 			case n := <-input:
671: 				if n == nil {
672: 					log.Debugf("Worker %d terminating...", i)
673: 					return
674: 				}
675: 
676: 				err := n.Delete(ctx)
677: 				if err != nil {
678: 					log.Errorf("could not remove container %q: %v", n.Config().LongName, err)
679: 				}
680: 			case <-ctx.Done():
681: 				return
682: 			}
683: 		}
684: 	}
685: 
686: 	// start concurrent workers
687: 	wg.Add(int(workers))
688: 
689: 	for i := range workers {
690: 		go workerFunc(i, concurrentChan, wg)
691: 	}
692: 
693: 	// send nodes to workers
694: 	for _, n := range c.Nodes {
695: 		concurrentChan <- n
696: 	}
697: 
698: 	// close channel to terminate the workers
699: 	close(concurrentChan)
700: 
701: 	wg.Wait()
702: 
703: 	// Host and mgmt-br links must be removed only after node workers finish.
704: 	// vxlan-stitch teardown deletes veth pairs from containers concurrently with
705: 	// host-namespace VXLAN interfaces; running both at once races netlink deletes.
706: 	c.deleteSpecialLinkNodes(ctx)
707: }
708: 
709: func (c *CLab) deleteSpecialLinkNodes(ctx context.Context) {
710: 	specialNodes := append(
711: 		[]clablinks.Node{clablinks.GetHostLinkNode()},
712: 		clablinks.GetMgmtBrLinkNodes()...,
713: 	)
714: 	for _, n := range specialNodes {
715: 		err := n.Delete(ctx)
716: 		if err != nil {
717: 			log.Warn(err)
718: 		}
719: 	}
720: }
721: 
722: // deleteContainersDirect deletes containers directly via the runtime API.
723: // This is used when we don't have node objects (e.g., destroy-by-name-only case).
724: func (c *CLab) deleteContainersDirect(
725: 	ctx context.Context,
726: 	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,
727: ) {
728: 	for _, cont := range containers {
729: 		if len(cont.Names) == 0 {
730: 			log.Warnf("skipping container %s with no names", cont.ID)
731: 			continue
732: 		}
733: 		name := cont.Names[0]
734: 		log.Infof("Removing container: %s", name)
735: 
736: 		c.logoutTailscaleContainer(ctx, cont)
737: 		rt := cont.Runtime
738: 		if rt == nil {
739: 			rt = c.globalRuntime()
740: 		}
741: 		err := rt.DeleteContainer(ctx, name)
742: 		if err != nil {
743: 			log.Errorf("could not remove container %q: %v", name, err)
744: 		}
745: 	}
746: }
747: 
748: func (c *CLab) deleteToolContainers(ctx context.Context) {
749: 	toolTypes := []string{"sshx", "gotty"}
750: 
751: 	if len(c.nodeFilter) == 0 {
752: 		toolTypes = append(toolTypes, clabnodestailscale.ToolType)
753: 	}
754: 
755: 	for _, toolType := range toolTypes {
756: 		toolFilter := []*clabtypes.GenericFilter{
757: 			{
758: 				FilterType: "label",
759: 				Field:      clabconstants.ToolType,
760: 				Operator:   "=",
761: 				Match:      toolType,
762: 			},
763: 			{
764: 				FilterType: "label",
765: 				Field:      clabconstants.Containerlab,
766: 				Operator:   "=",
767: 				Match:      c.Config.Name,
768: 			},
769: 		}
770: 
771: 		containers, err := c.globalRuntime().ListContainers(ctx, toolFilter)
772: 		if err != nil {
773: 			log.Error("Failed to list tool containers", "tool", toolType, "error", err)
774: 			continue
775: 		}
776: 
777: 		if len(containers) == 0 {
778: 			log.Debug("No tool containers found for lab", "tool", toolType, "lab", c.Config.Name)
779: 			continue
780: 		}
781: 
782: 		log.Info("Found tool containers associated with a lab", "tool", toolType, "lab",
783: 			c.Config.Name, "count", len(containers))
784: 
785: 		for idx := range containers {
786: 			containerName := strings.TrimPrefix(containers[idx].Names[0], "/")
787: 
788: 			log.Info("Removing tool container", "tool", toolType, "container", containerName)
789: 
790: 			c.logoutTailscaleContainer(ctx, containers[idx])
791: 			if err := c.globalRuntime().DeleteContainer(ctx, containerName); err != nil {
792: 				log.Error("Failed to remove tool container", "tool", toolType,
793: 					"container", containerName, "error", err)
794: 			} else {
795: 				log.Info(
796: 					"Tool container removed successfully",
797: 					"tool",
798: 					toolType,
799: 					"container",
800: 					containerName,
801: 				)
802: 			}
803: 		}
804: 	}
805: }
806: 
807: func cliPromptToDestroyAll(topos map[string]string) error {
808: 	var sb strings.Builder
809: 
810: 	idx := 1
811: 
812: 	for topo, labDir := range topos {
813: 		sb.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf("  %d. Topology: %s\n     Lab Dir: %s\n", idx, topo, labDir))
814: 		idx++
815: 	}
816: 
817: 	log.Warn("The following labs will be removed:", "labs", sb.String())
818: 
819: 	// red color (ansi code 1)
820: 	warningStyle := lipgloss.NewStyle().Bold(true).Foreground(lipgloss.Color("1"))
821: 	prompt := "Are you sure you want to remove all labs listed above? Enter 'y'," +
822: 		" to confirm or ENTER to abort: "
823: 	fmt.Print(warningStyle.Render(prompt))
824: 
825: 	reader := bufio.NewReader(os.Stdin)
826: 
827: 	input, err := reader.ReadString('\n')
828: 	if err != nil {
829: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to read user input: %v", err)
830: 	}
831: 
832: 	answer := strings.TrimSpace(input)
833: 	if answer == "" {
834: 		return errors.New("aborted by the user. No labs were removed")
835: 	}
836: 
837: 	answer = strings.ToLower(answer)
838: 	if answer != "y" && answer != "yes" {
839: 		return errors.New("aborted by the user. No labs were removed")
840: 	}
841: 
842: 	return nil
843: }

Directories\n// recorded in failedLabDirs are kept: their labs are still running, and the\n// directory holds the lab state a later destroy attempt relies on.\nfunc (c *CLab) destroyLabDirs(\n\ttopos map[string]string,\n\tall bool,\n\tfailedLabDirs map[string]bool,\n) error {\n\tif len(topos) == 0 {\n\t\tlog.Info(\"no containerlab containers found\")\n\n\t\tif !all {\n\t\t\tlabDir := c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()\n\t\t\tif labDir == \"\" {\n\t\t\t\t// Try to infer the precise lab directory when TopologyLabDir is not set,\n\t\t\t\t// e.g. destroy --name without topology file. <content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Clean up only your own mess.**\n38: \n39: When editing existing code:\n40: \n41: - Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n45: \n46: When your changes create orphans:\n47: \n48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n50: \n51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n52: \n53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n54: \n55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n56: \n57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:\n58: \n59: - \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n60: - \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n61: - \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n62: \n63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n64: \n65: ```\n66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n69: ```\n70: \n71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n72: \n73: ---\n74: \n75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.\n\n(End of file - total 75 lines)\n</content>",

        "text": "---\ndescription: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Karpathy behavioral guidelines\n\nBehavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.\n\n**Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.\n\n## 1. Think Before Coding\n\n**Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**\n\nBefore implementing:\n\n- State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.\n- If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n- If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.\n- If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.\n\n## 2. Simplicity First\n\n**Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**\n\n- No features beyond what was asked.\n- No abstractions for single-use code.\n- No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n- No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n- If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n\nAsk yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n\n## 3. Surgical Changes\n\n**Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n\nWhen editing existing code:\n\n- Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n\nWhen your changes create orphans:\n\n- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n\nThe test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n\n## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n\n**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n\nTransform tasks into verifiable goals:\n\n- \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n- \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n- \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n\nFor multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n\n```\n1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n```\n\nStrong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n\n---\n\n**These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.",

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

657: func (c *CLab) deleteNodes(ctx context.Context, workers uint) {
658: 	wg := new(sync.WaitGroup)
659: 
660: 	concurrentChan := make(chan clabnodes.Node)
661: 
662: 	workerFunc := func(i uint, input chan clabnodes.Node, wg *sync.WaitGroup) {
663: 		defer wg.Done()
664: 
665: 		for {
666: 			select {
667: 			case n := <-input:
668: 				if n == nil {
669: 					log.Debugf("Worker %d terminating...", i)
670: 					return
671: 				}
672:

674: 				if err != nil {
675: 					log.Errorf("could not remove container %q: %v", n.Config().LongName, err)

678: 				return
679: 			}

693: 	}

Reasoning
Now I understand the change. Key points:

1. In `destroy()`, when `len(containers) == 0` but `len(c.Nodes) > 0 && len(c.Links) > 0`, call `c.deleteSpecialLinkNodes(ctx)`. This handles the case where all containers are already gone but the host link node / mgmt-br link nodes still exist (e.g. after crash, partial destroy).

2. In `deleteNodes()`, `wg.Wait()` moved before the special link node deletion, and special node deletion extracted into `deleteSpecialLinkNodes`. Previously, special nodes were deleted concurrently with workers (before `wg.Wait()`), which races.

Now check details:

- `c.Links` — is it populated on destroy path? In `makeCopyForDestroy`, `ResolveLinks()` is called, which presumably populates `c.Links`. Let me check `ResolveLinks`. Also, in destroy-by-name-only path (`c.Nodes` empty), `c.Links` would be empty.

- What are special link nodes? `GetHostLinkNode()` and `GetMgmtBrLinkNodes()`. Let me look at the links package. Their Delete probably removes host links recorded in state, and mgmt-br links. If a lab's containers are all gone but host links remain, this new code cleans them up. But note the new call in destroy only happens when `len(c.Links) > 0`. If containers are gone and links empty, nothing. But what about when containers exist? Then deleteNodes calls deleteSpecialLinkNodes anyway (after wg.Wait). OK.

Potential issues:

1. Double invocation: when `len(containers) == 0` but the destroy path also has `len(c.Nodes) > 0`... wait no — if containers==0, the function returns early at line 426-427, so deleteNodes isn't reached. Good.

2. But note: if `len(c.Nodes) > 0 && len(c.Links) > 0` and containers exist — deleteNodes → deleteSpecialLinkNodes runs. Fine.

3. What if containers == 0 and `c.Links` is empty but host links exist? `c.Links` populated by ResolveLinks based on topology file. If topology file is present, links resolve. If the topology has no links but host links exist... host links only exist if they were created, meaning topology had host links. Hmm — but consider: destroy with node filter that filters all nodes → c.Nodes filtered? Let me check filterClabNodes. Actually if nodeFilter filters out all nodes, `c.Nodes` may be empty → the branch doesn't run and CleanupFilteredLinks handles filtered links. Also, previously (base), when containers == 0, nothing was deleted — including special nodes. This change fixes orphaned host/mgmt-br links after all containers already removed (e.g., a partially failed destroy, or the vxlan-stitch case).

4. Concurrency: In base, special nodes were deleted concurrently with worker goroutines — race. Now fixed by wg.Wait() before deleteSpecialLinkNodes. Good improvement.

5. `deleteNodes` — but wait, there's a subtlety: `wg.Wait()` moved before `close(concurrentChan)`? No: close happens at line 699, then wg.Wait() at 701, then deleteSpecialLinkNodes at 706. Correct order.

But hold on — in base, `wg.Wait()` was after special node deletion; if the channel closed and workers finished, wg.Wait in base also ensured workers done. The old order: close → special deletes → wg.Wait. So special deletes ran concurrently with workers — the race the PR fixes. New order is correct.

6. Now the new early-return call: is `c.Links` guaranteed populated? In `makeCopyForDestroy`, `ResolveLinks()` is called. But there are other callers of `c.destroy(...)`? Let me grep for `c.destroy(` in head.

Also check: does `deleteSpecialLinkNodes` do anything harmful when there are no special links? `GetHostLinkNode().Delete(ctx)` and mgmt-br link nodes Delete — they probably check state for links to delete. Let me look at links package.

Also the condition `len(c.Links) > 0`: if a topology has links but c.Links resolution failed/skipped... Actually in destroy-by-name-only, c.Nodes == 0, so skipped. If nodes exist but links resolve to zero (topology with only mgmt links?), mgmt-br link nodes come from `GetMgmtBrLinkNodes()` — are those derived from c.Links or from mgmt network config? Let me look.

Let me grep the links package.
Now let me examine the links package to understand what the special link nodes are and how `Delete` behaves.

51: func (g *GenericLinkNode) AdoptEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
52: 	if e == nil {
53: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q cannot adopt a nil endpoint", g.shortname)
54: 	}
55: 
56: 	owner := e.GetNode()
57: 	if owner == nil {
58: 		return fmt.Errorf(
59: 			"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q without an owner",
60: 			g.shortname,
61: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
62: 		)
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	if owner.GetShortName() != g.shortname {
66: 		return fmt.Errorf(
67: 			"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q owned by %q",
68: 			g.shortname,
69: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
70: 			owner.GetShortName(),
71: 		)
72: 	}
73: 
74: 	for _, owned := range g.endpoints {
75: 		if owned == e {
76: 			return nil
77: 		}
78: 		if owned.GetIfaceName() == e.GetIfaceName() {
79: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q already tracks interface %q", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())
80: 		}
81: 	}
82: 
83: 	g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints, e)
84: 	return nil
85: }
86: 
87: func (g *GenericLinkNode) ReleaseEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
88: 	for i, ep := range g.endpoints {
89: 		if ep != e {
90: 			continue
91: 		}
92: 
93: 		g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints[:i], g.endpoints[i+1:]...)
94: 		return nil
95: 	}
96: 
97: 	return fmt.Errorf("node %q does not own endpoint %q", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())
98: }
99: 
100: func (g *GenericLinkNode) GetShortName() string {
101: 	return g.shortname
102: }
103: 
104: func (g *GenericLinkNode) GetEndpoints() []Endpoint {
105: 	return g.endpoints
106: }
107: 
108: func (*GenericLinkNode) GetState() clabnodesstate.NodeState {
109: 	// The GenericLinkNode is the basis for Mgmt-Bridge and Host fake node.
110: 	// Both of these do generally exist. Hence the Deployed state in generally returned
111: 	return clabnodesstate.Deployed
112: }
113: 
114: func (g *GenericLinkNode) Delete(ctx context.Context) error {
115: 	for _, e := range g.endpoints {
116: 		var err error
117: 		if l := e.GetLink(); l != nil {
118: 			err = l.Remove(ctx)
119: 		} else {
120: 			err = e.Remove(ctx)
121: 		}
122: 		if err != nil {
123: 			return err
124: 		}
125: 	}
126: 	return nil
127: }

(End of file - total 127 lines)

Reasoning
`GenericLinkNode.Delete` iterates over endpoints attached to the special node. These endpoints come from link resolution (`hostEp.GetNode().AddEndpoint(hostEp)` at link resolve time). So in the new early-return path in `destroy()`, whether `deleteSpecialLinkNodes` actually removes anything depends on `c.Links` being populated AND the host/mgmt-br endpoints being attached to the singleton link nodes during `ResolveLinks()`.

Now, are endpoints added to the special node's `endpoints` slice during ResolveLinks, even in the destroy copy? Yes — `LinkHostRaw.Resolve` calls `hostEp.GetNode().AddEndpoint(hostEp)`. But: the host link node is a package-level singleton. In a destroy copy, `ResolveLinks` re-resolves the topology, adding endpoints to the singleton. This is pre-existing behavior (base's `deleteNodes` also relies on it). But in base, when `len(containers) == 0`, `destroy()` returned early and never deleted special nodes — orphaned host links (e.g. veth pairs attached to host) would linger. The PR fixes that.

Potential issue #1: The condition `len(c.Links) > 0` — is `c.Links` populated in all cases where `c.Nodes > 0`? Let me check `ResolveLinks`. In `makeCopyForDestroy`, `ResolveLinks` is called unconditionally. But there's a case: `destroy()` invoked from `core/deploy.go` line 198 (`_ = c.destroy(ctx, uint(len(c.Nodes)), true)`) — that's in some rollback path. Does that CLab have Links populated? Let's look.

Also, `filterClabNodes` — when a node filter is applied, are `c.Links` filtered? `CleanupFilteredLinks` handles filtered links separately. Hmm, if node filter excludes all nodes, then `c.Nodes` might still contain all nodes? Let me check filterClabNodes in clab.go.

Potential issue #2: `GetMgmtBrLinkNodes()` returns the mgmt-br link nodes "that have been referenced so far" — package-level global registry `_mgmtBrLinkNodes`. In a single process, if destroying multiple labs (destroy --all loops over topos sequentially), the singleton registry persists across labs. `deleteSpecialLinkNodes` deletes endpoints attached to the singletons. But each lab's copy re-resolves its own links and adds endpoints to the shared singletons... Actually wait — `getMgmtBrLinkNode(network)` returns the same node instance for the same network name across labs. And `SetMgmtNetworkUnderlyingBridge` sets shortname to the bridge name. So for lab A, the mgmt-br node's shortname becomes "br-..." for lab A. For lab B with different bridge, the same node's shortname is mutated. That's pre-existing behavior though.

But importantly, endpoints attached to the singleton: `AddEndpoint` appends. When destroy of lab A runs `deleteSpecialLinkNodes`, it deletes lab A's endpoints from the singleton. Then lab B resolve adds its endpoints. Since destroy is sequential per lab (in the for loop over topos), this should be OK. Actually wait — is it? In base, when lab A has containers and lab B has containers, each lab's deleteNodes deletes special nodes concurrently. Pre-existing.

Hmm, but the new early-return path: lab A has zero containers (all already gone) but Links > 0 → deleteSpecialLinkNodes called. This deletes the singleton's endpoints, which may include endpoints from a previously-resolved lab? No — each `makeCopyForDestroy` creates a NEW CLab, resolves links, adding endpoints to the singleton. But previous lab's endpoints were deleted (Remove) during that lab's destroy... but if the previous lab's destroy took the early-return path with no links... endpoints accumulate? Actually endpoints are only removed when Delete is called, and Delete removes each endpoint's link (deleting veth). If a lab's destroy never calls deleteSpecialLinkNodes (e.g., base behavior when containers==0), the singleton accumulates endpoints from that resolve. Then a later lab's deleteSpecialLinkNodes would try to delete the earlier lab's host interfaces too — but those interfaces no longer exist, so Remove would error → log.Warn. Hmm, that's pre-existing accumulation, not introduced.

Wait, actually — is it pre-existing? In base, when containers == 0, deleteSpecialLinkNodes was never called, so singleton accumulation happened but never caused cross-lab deletion because... actually in base with two labs where lab A ends with containers==0 (never delete special nodes) and lab B has containers: lab B's deleteNodes → specialNodes delete iterates endpoints including lab A's stale endpoints (added when lab A's copy resolved links). So yes, pre-existing accumulation issue. The PR's new call actually mitigates this for the early-return case by deleting the endpoints. Hmm, but only when `len(c.Links) > 0`.

Now, more important issue: does the new early path handle the case where `len(c.Links) == 0` but special links exist? E.g., destroy where topology file is missing (WithLabNameOnly) — then c.Nodes is empty too, so the guard fails anyway. If topology exists and has no host/mgmt links, c.Links would contain only non-special links; deleting special nodes with zero endpoints is a no-op (Delete iterates empty endpoints). So calling deleteSpecialLinkNodes unconditionally (when len(c.Nodes)>0) would be simpler and equally safe. The `len(c.Links) > 0` condition is a bit arbitrary but harmless. Hmm — actually is it harmless? If c.Links is empty but c.Nodes > 0 and the singleton mgmt-br node has endpoints from a prior lab in the same process (destroy --all), the stale endpoints would be deleted... but that's arguably correct cleanup (Remove will fail or succeed). Wait — cross-lab deletion of another lab's host veth would be BAD: lab A still running with host links; lab B destroy with containers==0 and Links==0... no wait, if lab B's c.Links == 0, the guard skips deleteSpecialLinkNodes. Good. But if lab B's c.Links > 0, deleteSpecialLinkNodes iterates ALL endpoints on the singleton including lab A's live host veths → deletes lab A's host interfaces! Is that possible?

Scenario: `containerlab destroy --all` with labs A and B. Lab A resolved first (its copy), destroyed (deletes its special endpoints). Lab B resolved second, destroyed. Sequential, so singletons only carry lab B's endpoints at lab B's delete time. But wait — lab A's endpoints on the singleton: after lab A's destroy, `Delete` on host node iterates endpoints and calls Remove on each. Does Remove remove the endpoint from the node's endpoints slice? Let's check. `l.Remove(ctx)` for LinkVEth — probably deletes the veth pair. The endpoint stays in g.endpoints slice. So on lab B's delete, lab A's stale endpoints are deleted AGAIN — but the veth already gone, Remove fails → log.Warn (only Warn, not fatal). OK, pre-existing noise.

But the dangerous cross-lab case: lab A still RUNNING (destroy of lab A failed or lab A's destroy path returned early at containers==0 without deleting special nodes... hmm, containers==0 means no containers running for lab A per the filter).

Hmm wait, actually there's a REAL cross-lab scenario: destroy --all where lab A destroy FAILS midway (deleteNodes errors, wg done, deleteSpecialLinkNodes runs though...). Actually deleteSpecialLinkNodes is always called at the end of deleteNodes in the new code, even on error paths within workers (errors are just logged). So after lab A's destroy, singleton endpoints for lab A remain in the slice (Remove failed or succeeded but slice not pruned). Lab B's resolve adds lab B's endpoints. Lab B's deleteSpecialLinkNodes deletes lab B's endpoints AND retries lab A's stale endpoints (harmless no-ops or warns). Not live deletion of lab A's interfaces because lab A's deletes already attempted. Unless lab A's destroy never reached deleteSpecialLinkNodes (early return `if err != nil return err` after CleanupFilteredLinks failure, or the new containers==0 early return before PR... in the new code that path now calls deleteSpecialLinkNodes too when Links>0).

Hmm, one more: in base, the pre-existing flow already had this cross-lab singleton accumulation. So the PR doesn't worsen it materially. The PR actually makes things more consistent.

Now the ordering fix in deleteNodes: wg.Wait() before special deletes. Correct and matches the comment. Good.

Now, key question: does the new early-return call introduce double-deletion or missed cases?

Case: `len(containers) == 0` and `len(c.Nodes) > 0` and `len(c.Links) > 0` → deleteSpecialLinkNodes. Good — cleans orphaned host veths.

Case: `len(containers) == 0`, `len(c.Nodes) > 0`, `len(c.Links) == 0` → skip. But could there be special link endpoints even with c.Links empty? c.Links empty means ResolveLinks produced no links; endpoints only get attached to special nodes during link resolution, so no endpoints → no-op. Fine.

Hmm wait, but is `c.Links` really the resolved links? Let me check the CLab struct and ResolveLinks. Also check `filterClabNodes` — with a node filter that filters out all nodes: c.Nodes would be empty? Let me check.

Now, `filterClabNodes` deletes filtered-out nodes from `c.Config.Topology.Nodes`, so `c.Nodes` only contains the filtered-in nodes. When a node filter excludes all nodes... the CLI probably validates? Let's not worry.

Now let me look at the CLab struct for `Links` field type and confirm the new code compiles (it's from the actual PR, so yes).

Let's also check `LinkVEth.Remove` and host endpoint Remove — to see if the endpoints handle deleting host-side veth. And `link_veth.go` Remove.

Also, one thing to verify: the comment in deleteNodes says "vxlan-stitch teardown deletes veth pairs from containers concurrently with host-namespace VXLAN interfaces". Let's check the vxlan link Remove path — probably `link_vxlan_stitch.go` or similar. Node Delete concurrently removes veth pairs whose peer is in host ns (vxlan stitched links); concurrently deleting host VXLAN interfaces races. The fix: serialize by waiting.

Now, potential issue with the new early-return call: `deleteSpecialLinkNodes` uses `GetMgmtBrLinkNodes()` which includes ALL mgmt-br link nodes registered globally — including those created for other labs in the same process (destroy --all with different mgmt networks). But endpoints attached are per-resolve. `resetResolvedLinks` releases only this CLab's endpoints... from the shared singleton nodes. Hmm, `ReleaseEndpoint` removes from the singleton's endpoint slice. So each copy's ResolveLinks first releases previous endpoints (but previous lab's endpoints aren't in this copy's c.Endpoints — only its own). Actually resetResolvedLinks only releases this copy's endpoints (c.Endpoints starts nil in a fresh copy). So singleton accumulates across labs. Pre-existing.

Now — cross-lab deletion concern in the early path: `destroy --all` where lab A still running (failed destroy) leaves its endpoints on the singleton with live host veths; then lab B destroy with containers==0 and Links>0 → deleteSpecialLinkNodes deletes lab B's endpoints AND lab A's stale endpoints → deletes lab A's host interfaces while lab A is still running!

Wait — can that happen? Lab A's destroy: if it has containers, deleteNodes → deleteSpecialLinkNodes deletes lab A's endpoints (Remove on each). If Remove fails, endpoint stays but link likely still exists... Actually if Remove failed, the interface may still exist. Then lab B's deleteSpecialLinkNodes re-attempts Remove on lab A's stale endpoint — if the veth still exists (Remove failed earlier transiently), it gets deleted → lab A's host link removed. Edge-casey. But this accumulation pattern is pre-existing in base too (base's deleteNodes deleted special nodes concurrently for each lab). So the PR doesn't introduce it. Not a finding unless the PR materially worsens. Actually the PR does add a new call site (early return) that can fire when a lab's containers are all gone but singleton holds stale endpoints from an earlier failed lab... marginal.

Hmm, but there's a subtler NEW issue: singleton `hostLinkNode` endpoints. In the new early path, `deleteSpecialLinkNodes` is invoked on the copy `cc` — but `GetHostLinkNode()` returns the process-wide singleton. `cc`'s resolve attached cc's endpoints. Fine.

Now, another important check: the condition uses `len(c.Links) > 0`. In the `--reconfigure` deploy path (deploy.go:198), `c.destroy(...)` is called with keepMgmtNet=true. If reconfigure is happening and all containers already gone (partial state), the new code deletes special nodes — good.

But WAIT: in the reconfigure path, `c` is the deploying CLab (not a copy), and `c.Links` was resolved with... fine.

Now potential problem: in `destroy()`, the early-return call happens BEFORE `CleanupFilteredLinks`. With a node filter where all filtered-in nodes' containers are absent but filtered-out nodes' containers still running: `len(containers)` — containers comes from `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` which lists only filtered-in nodes' containers? Let me check. If it lists only node containers of filtered nodes, then containers==0 → early return → special link nodes for filtered-in links deleted. Reasoning
Now let me check `nodes/default_node.go:474` — that's the node Delete removing its endpoints (veth pairs). That's what runs concurrently in deleteNodes workers. And the host/mgmt special nodes' Delete removes host-namespace VXLAN interfaces (VxlanStitched.Remove → vxlanLink.Remove → localEndpoint.Remove in host ns). So the race: workers deleting container-side veths (LinkVEth.Remove → both endpoints) concurrently with special node Delete removing VXLAN host interface. The vxlan-stitch link: `GetEndpoints` returns vxlan local/remote endpoints; the special host node's endpoints — which endpoint does the host node hold for a stitched vxlan? In `ResolveStitched`, `stitchEp` — let me check link_vxlan.go around line 50-133.

Actually the important bit: VxlanStitched.Remove deletes both veth (container side) and vxlan (host side). If node workers delete the veth concurrently with the host node Delete deleting the vxlan... but each component has its own mutex? LinkVEth.Remove takes l.deployMutex; LinkVxlan.Remove probably takes its own. Hmm, actually the ordering concern in the comment: node worker deletes veth (both ends) while host node deletes VXLAN — "running both at once races netlink deletes". Plausible: veth delete in container ns and vxlan delete in host ns race on the same underlying link pair? Whatever — the fix (wg.Wait before special deletes) is correct.

Let me quickly verify: in base, the special node deletion ran BEFORE wg.Wait — i.e., concurrently with workers. In head, wg.Wait() first. Good fix.

Now check the early-return path more carefully:

```go
if len(containers) == 0 {
    if len(c.Nodes) > 0 && len(c.Links) > 0 {
        c.deleteSpecialLinkNodes(ctx)
    }
    return nil
}
```

Wait — is `c.Links` a map? `c.Links = make(map[int]clablinks.Link)` — yes map. `len` works.

Now when is `destroy()` called with Nodes>0, containers==0? Cases:
- Lab fully destroyed already except special links remain (host veths, vxlan, mgmt-br veths? Actually mgmt-br endpoints are veth pairs with one end in host attached to mgmt bridge... wait, mgmt links attach container to bridge; the bridge is host-side. Hmm, `GetMgmtBrLinkNode` Delete → endpoints are the container-end endpoints? Let me think: for a mgmt link, the endpoints are (node, mgmt-br). The mgmt-br endpoint's link is a veth with container end. Deleting the endpoint removes the veth. So if containers are gone but the veth's host side was left dangling... deleting it cleans up.
- `--reconfigure` deploy: containers already gone (previous deploy failed after node creation? or partial).

The bug being fixed: previously, with containers==0, destroy returned without deleting host/mgmt-br special nodes → leftover host interfaces. Now fixed. Good.

Now, is there a case where `len(c.Nodes) > 0` but `len(c.Links) == 0` and special endpoints still exist? ResolveLinks attaches endpoints only for resolved (non-nil) links, and every non-nil link is stored in c.Links. So Links empty ⇒ no endpoints on special nodes from this lab ⇒ deleteSpecialLinkNodes would be a no-op (or delete stale endpoints from other labs). Actually — hmm, if Links==0 but singleton has stale endpoints from a previous lab in the same process, calling deleteSpecialLinkNodes unconditionally would attempt those deletes. The guard `len(c.Links) > 0` avoids that. But conversely, if Links>0 the singleton's stale endpoints from other labs WOULD be attempted — same as pre-existing behavior in deleteNodes. So consistent.

Now, are there tests for this? Let me check core/destroy_test.go or related.

168: // destroyTestBehavior configures the fake runtime the destroy tests register under the
169: // "destroytest" name. The behavior is read by every instance NewContainerLab creates for
170: // the duration of one test.
171: type destroyTestBehavior struct {
172: 	// containers is returned by the first ListContainers call.
173: 	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer
174: 	// failCreateNet makes CreateNet return an error, which fails makeCopyForDestroy.
175: 	failCreateNet bool
176: 	// listErrAfterFirst makes every ListContainers call after the first return an
177: 	// error, which fails the inner destroy after the copy was built.
178: 	listErrAfterFirst bool
179: 	// listEmptyAfterFirst makes every ListContainers call after the first return no
180: 	// containers, so the inner destroy completes without touching nodes.
181: 	listEmptyAfterFirst bool
182: }
183: 
184: var destroyTestRuntimeBehavior atomic.Pointer[destroyTestBehavior]
185: 
186: // destroyTestListCalls counts ListContainers calls across every fake runtime instance
187: // the destroy tests create: the first call is the outer Destroy inventory, the later
188: // ones belong to the CLab copy makeCopyForDestroy builds.
189: var destroyTestListCalls atomic.Int32
190: 
191: var registerDestroyTestRuntimeOnce sync.Once
192: 
193: // registerDestroyTestRuntime registers a minimal fake container runtime the Destroy
194: // unit tests can drive without a container engine.
195: func registerDestroyTestRuntime() {
196: 	registerDestroyTestRuntimeOnce.Do(func() {
197: 		clabruntime.Register("destroytest", func() clabruntime.ContainerRuntime {
198: 			return &fakeDestroyRuntime{}
199: 		})
200: 	})
201: }
202: 
203: // setDestroyTestBehavior installs the behavior for one test and resets the call
204: // bookkeeping the previous test left behind.
205: func setDestroyTestBehavior(t *testing.T, behavior *destroyTestBehavior) {
206: 	t.Helper()
207: 
208: 	destroyTestListCalls.Store(0)
209: 	destroyTestRuntimeBehavior.Store(behavior)
210: 
211: 	t.Cleanup(func() {
212: 		destroyTestRuntimeBehavior.Store(nil)
213: 	})
214: }

292: // writeDestroyTestTopology writes a minimal topology file and returns its path.
293: func writeDestroyTestTopology(t *testing.T, labName string) string {
294: 	t.Helper()
295: 
296: 	topoDir := t.TempDir()
297: 	topo := filepath.Join(topoDir, labName+".clab.yml")
298: 
299: 	const definition = `name: %s
300: topology:
301:   nodes:
302:     n1:
303:       kind: linux
304:       image: alpine:3
305: `
306: 
307: 	if err := os.WriteFile(topo, []byte(fmt.Sprintf(definition, labName)), 0o644); err != nil {
308: 		t.Fatal(err)
309: 	}
310: 
311: 	return topo
312: }

314: // TestDestroyCleanupReportsDestroyFailure reproduces issue 3467: with --cleanup a failed
315: // destroy must not be reported as success and must not delete the lab directory of a lab
316: // whose destroy failed.
317: func TestDestroyCleanupReportsDestroyFailure(t *testing.T) {
318: 	registerDestroyTestRuntime()
319: 
320: 	for _, tc := range []struct {
321: 		name              string
322: 		failCreateNet     bool
323: 		listErrAfterFirst bool
324: 	}{
325: 		{name: "makeCopyForDestroy fails", failCreateNet: true},
326: 		{name: "destroy fails after the copy was built", listErrAfterFirst: true},
327: 	} {
328: 		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
329: 			topo := writeDestroyTestTopology(t, "ovr")
330: 			labDir := t.TempDir()
331: 
332: 			behavior := &destroyTestBehavior{
333: 				containers: []clabruntime.GenericContainer{{
334: 					Names: []string{"clab-ovr-n1"},
335: 					Labels: map[string]string{
336: 						clabconstants.TopoFile:   topo,
337: 						clabconstants.NodeLabDir: labDir,
338: 					},
339: 				}},
340: 				failCreateNet:     tc.failCreateNet,
341: 				listErrAfterFirst: tc.listErrAfterFirst,
342: 			}
343: 			setDestroyTestBehavior(t, behavior)
344: 
345: 			c, err := NewContainerLab(
346: 				WithTimeout(time.Minute),
347: 				WithTopoPath(topo, nil),
348: 				WithRuntime("destroytest", &clabruntime.RuntimeConfig{}),
349: 				WithSkippedBindsPathsCheck(),
350: 			)
351: 			if err != nil {
352: 				t.Fatal(err)
353: 			}
354: 
355: 			err = c.Destroy(
356: 				context.Background(),
357: 				WithDestroyMaxWorkers(1),
358: 				WithDestroyCleanup(),
359: 			)
360: 			if err == nil {
361: 				t.Fatal("destroy reported success although the destroy failed")
362: 			}
363: 
364: 			if _, statErr := os.Stat(labDir); statErr != nil {
365: 				t.Fatalf("lab directory of the failed destroy was removed: %v", statErr)
366: 			}
367: 		})
368: 	}
369: }

371: // TestDestroyCleanupRemovesLabDirsOnSuccess checks that --cleanup keeps removing lab
372: // directories of labs that were destroyed successfully.
373: func TestDestroyCleanupRemovesLabDirsOnSuccess(t *testing.T) {
374: 	registerDestroyTestRuntime()
375: 	withTestHostsFiles(t, t.TempDir())
376: 
377: 	topo := writeDestroyTestTopology(t, "ok")
378: 	labDir := t.TempDir()
379:

381: 		containers: []clabruntime.GenericContainer{{
382: 			Names: []string{"clab-ok-n1"},

385: 				clabconstants.NodeLabDir: labDir,
386: 			},
387: 		}},
388: 		listEmptyAfterFirst: true,
389: 	}

394: 		WithTopoPath(topo, nil),

415: // TestDestroyLabDirsKeepsFailedLabDirs checks destroyLabDirs directly: only the lab
416: // directories of successful destroys are removed.
417: func TestDestroyLabDirsKeepsFailedLabDirs(t *testing.T) {
418: 	t.Parallel()
419: 
420: 	removed := t.TempDir()
421: 	kept := t.TempDir()
422: 
423: 	c := &CLab{}
424:

427: 			"a.clab.yml": removed,
428: 			"b.clab.yml": kept,

432: 	)

434: 		t.Fatal(err)
435: 	}

439: 	}

441: 		t.Fatalf("lab directory of the failed destroy was removed: %v", statErr)
442: 	}

445: // TestDestroyWithNoContainersRemovesOnlyOwnLabDir reproduces GitHub issue #3499:
446: // `clab destroy --cleanup` with no running containers deleted an arbitrary clab-*
447: // directory (the first glob match) instead of the lab's own directory.
448: func TestDestroyWithNoContainersRemovesOnlyOwnLabDir(t *testing.T) {
449: 	t.Parallel()
450: 
451: 	topoDir := t.TempDir()
452: 	topoName := "vyos-clos"
453: 	topoPath := filepath.Join(topoDir, topoName+".clab.yml")
454: 	const definition = `name: %s
455: topology:
456:   nodes:
457:     n1:
458:       kind: linux
459:       image: alpine:3
460: `
461: 	if err := os.WriteFile(topoPath, []byte(fmt.Sprintf(definition, topoName)), 0o644); err != nil {
462: 		t.Fatal(err)
463: 	}
464: 
465: 	// Simulate the situation from the bug report: several clab-* directories exist
466: 	// next to the topology file, including unrelated ones.
467: 	for _, dir := range []string{"clab-clos01", "clab-docs", "clab-vyos-clos", "clab-mikrotik-vyos"} {
468: 		if err := os.MkdirAll(filepath.Join(topoDir, dir), 0o755); err != nil {
469: 			t.Fatal(err)
470: 		}
471: 	}
472: 	// Sentinel files to prove which dirs survived.
473: 	for _, dir := range []string{"clab-clos01", "clab-docs", "clab-mikrotik-vyos", "clab-vyos-clos"} {
474: 		if err := os.WriteFile(
475: 			filepath.Join(topoDir, dir, "sentinel"),
476: 			[]byte(dir),
477: 			0o644,
478: 		); err != nil {
479: 			t.Fatal(err)
480: 		}
481: 	}
482: 
483: 	c, err := NewContainerLab(
484: 		WithTimeout(time.Minute),
485: 		WithTopoPath(topoPath, nil),
486: 		WithSkippedBindsPathsCheck(),
487: 	)
488: 	if err != nil {
489: 		t.Fatal(err)
490: 	}
491: 	if c.Config.Name != topoName {
492: 		t.Fatalf("Config.Name = %q, want %q", c.Config.Name, topoName)
493: 	}
494: 	expectedLabDir := filepath.Join(topoDir, "clab-"+topoName)
495: 	if got := c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir(); got != expectedLabDir {
496: 		t.Fatalf("TopologyLabDir = %q, want %q", got, expectedLabDir)
497: 	}
498: 
499: 	// No containers -> empty topos map triggers the fallback branch.
500: 	if err := c.destroyLabDirs(map[string]string{}, false, map[string]bool{}); err != nil {
501: 		t.Fatalf("destroyLabDirs failed: %v", err)
502: 	}
503: 
504: 	// Only the expected lab directory should be removed.
505: 	if _, err := os.Stat(expectedLabDir); !os.IsNotExist(err) {
506: 		t.Fatalf("expected lab dir %q to be removed, stat: %v", expectedLabDir, err)
507: 	}
508: 	for _, dir := range []string{"clab-clos01", "clab-docs", "clab-mikrotik-vyos"} {
509: 		p := filepath.Join(topoDir, dir)
510: 		if _, err := os.Stat(p); err != nil {
511: 			t.Fatalf("unrelated dir %q should not have been removed: %v", p, err)
512: 		}
513: 	}
514: }

668: 
669: // mgmtResolveBehavior configures the fake runtime the management network resolution
670: // tests register under the "mgmtresolvetest" name.
671: type mgmtResolveBehavior struct {
672: 	// checkable lets the fake implement the MgmtNetworkChecker and MgmtBridgeResolver
673: 	// interfaces.
674: 	checkable bool
675: 	// networkExists is what MgmtNetworkExists and ResolveMgmtBridge report when
676: 	// checkable is set.
677: 	networkExists bool
678: 	// failCreateNet makes CreateNet return an error, so a call to it is observable
679: 	// through the test failing instead of silently creating a network.
680: 	failCreateNet bool
681: }
682: 
683: var mgmtResolveRuntimeBehavior atomic.Pointer[mgmtResolveBehavior]
684: 
685: // mgmtResolveRuntimeStats aggregates the calls every fake instance of one test makes.
686: type mgmtResolveRuntimeStats struct {
687: 	createNetCalls atomic.Int32
688: 	existsCalls    atomic.Int32
689: 	resolveCalls   atomic.Int32
690: }
691: 
692: var mgmtResolveRuntimeStatsPtr atomic.Pointer[mgmtResolveRuntimeStats]
693: 
694: var registerMgmtResolveRuntimeOnce sync.Once
695: 
696: func registerMgmtResolveRuntime() {
697: 	registerMgmtResolveRuntimeOnce.Do(func() {
698: 		clabruntime.Register("mgmtresolvetest", func() clabruntime.ContainerRuntime {
699: 			behavior := mgmtResolveRuntimeBehavior.Load()
700: 			if behavior != nil && behavior.checkable {
701: 				return &fakeMgmtResolveCheckableRuntime{}
702: 			}
703: 
704: 			return &fakeMgmtResolveRuntime{}
705: 		})
706: 	})
707: }
708: 
709: // setMgmtResolveBehavior installs the behavior for one test and resets the call
710: // bookkeeping the previous test left behind.
711: func setMgmtResolveBehavior(t *testing.T, behavior *mgmtResolveBehavior) {
712: 	t.Helper()
713: 
714: 	mgmtResolveRuntimeStatsPtr.Store(&mgmtResolveRuntimeStats{})
715: 	mgmtResolveRuntimeBehavior.Store(behavior)
716: 
717: 	t.Cleanup(func() {
718: 		mgmtResolveRuntimeBehavior.Store(nil)
719: 	})
720: }
721: 
722: type fakeMgmtResolveRuntime struct {
723: 	clabruntime.ContainerRuntime
724: 	mgmt   *clabtypes.MgmtNet
725: 	config *clabruntime.RuntimeConfig
726: }
727: 
728: func (f *fakeMgmtResolveRuntime) Init(opts ...clabruntime.RuntimeOption) error {
729: 	for _, opt := range opts {
730: 		opt(f)
731: 	}
732: 
733: 	return nil
734: }
735: 
736: func (f *fakeMgmtResolveRuntime) WithConfig(cfg *clabruntime.RuntimeConfig) { f.config = cfg }
737: 
738: func (f *fakeMgmtResolveRuntime) WithMgmtNet(mgmt *clabtypes.MgmtNet) { f.mgmt = mgmt }
739: 
740: func (f *fakeMgmtResolveRuntime) Mgmt() *clabtypes.MgmtNet { return f.mgmt }
741: 
742: func (*fakeMgmtResolveRuntime) GetName() string { return "mgmtresolvetest" }
743: 
744: func (f *fakeMgmtResolveRuntime) Config() clabruntime.RuntimeConfig {
745: 	if f.config == nil {
746: 		return clabruntime.RuntimeConfig{}
747: 	}
748: 
749: 	return *f.config
750: }
751: 
752: func (*fakeMgmtResolveRuntime) GetRuntimeSocket() (string, error) { return "", nil }
753: 
754: func (f *fakeMgmtResolveRuntime) CreateNet(
755: 	context.Context,
756: 	...clabruntime.NetworkCreateOptions,
757: ) error {
758: 	stats := mgmtResolveRuntimeStatsPtr.Load()
759: 	if stats != nil {
760: 		stats.createNetCalls.Add(1)
761: 	}
762: 
763: 	behavior := mgmtResolveRuntimeBehavior.Load()
764: 	if behavior != nil && behavior.failCreateNet {
765: 		return errors.New("synthetic network creation failure")
766: 	}
767: 
768: 	// the real runtimes resolve the bridge of an existing network; mimic it so the
769: 	// test can observe that destroy kept the resolution step
770: 	if f.mgmt != nil && f.mgmt.Bridge == "" {
771: 		f.mgmt.Bridge = "br-fake"
772: 	}
773: 
774: 	return nil
775: }
776: 
777: // fakeMgmtResolveCheckableRuntime is the variant of the fake runtime that implements
778: // the MgmtNetworkChecker interface.
779: type fakeMgmtResolveCheckableRuntime struct {
780: 	fakeMgmtResolveRuntime
781: }
782: 
783: // ForMgmtNet binds a copy of the fake to another management network, so the fake
784: // supports the multiple management networks of a topology.
785: func (f *fakeMgmtResolveCheckableRuntime) ForMgmtNet(
786: 	m *clabtypes.MgmtNet,
787: ) clabruntime.ContainerRuntime {
788: 	c := *f
789: 	c.mgmt = m
790: 	return &c
791: }
792: 
793: func (*fakeMgmtResolveCheckableRuntime) MgmtNetworkExists(context.Context) (bool, error) {
794: 	stats := mgmtResolveRuntimeStatsPtr.Load()
795: 	if stats != nil {
796: 		stats.existsCalls.Add(1)
797: 	}
798: 
799: 	behavior := mgmtResolveRuntimeBehavior.Load()
800: 	if behavior == nil {
801: 		return false, nil
802: 	}
803: 
804: 	return behavior.networkExists, nil
805: }
806: 
807: func (f *fakeMgmtResolveCheckableRuntime) ResolveMgmtBridge(context.Context) error {
808: 	stats := mgmtResolveRuntimeStatsPtr.Load()
809: 	if stats != nil {
810: 		stats.resolveCalls.Add(1)
811: 	}
812: 
813: 	behavior := mgmtResolveRuntimeBehavior.Load()
814: 	if behavior != nil && behavior.networkExists && f.mgmt.Bridge == "" {
815: 		f.mgmt.Bridge = "br-fake"
816: 	}
817: 
818: 	return nil
819: }
820: 
821: // TestMakeCopyForDestroySkipsMissingMgmtNetworks reproduces issue 3466: destroying a
822: // lab that was deployed with a management network override must not fail because the
823: // topology's default network does not exist and its default subnets are taken by the
824: // overridden network.
825: func TestMakeCopyForDestroySkipsMissingMgmtNetworks(t *testing.T) {
826: 	registerMgmtResolveRuntime()
827: 
828: 	topo := writeDestroyTestTopology(t, "ovr")
829: 
830: 	for _, tc := range []struct {
831: 		name          string
832: 		checkable     bool
833: 		networkExists bool
834: 		failCreateNet bool
835: 		wantExists    bool
836: 		wantCreateNet bool
837: 	}{
838: 		{
839: 			name:          "missing network is skipped",
840: 			checkable:     true,
841: 			networkExists: false,
842: 			failCreateNet: true,
843: 			wantExists:    true,
844: 			wantCreateNet: false,
845: 		},
846: 		{
847: 			name:          "existing network is resolved",
848: 			checkable:     true,
849: 			networkExists: true,
850: 			failCreateNet: false,
851: 			wantExists:    true,
852: 			wantCreateNet: true,
853: 		},
854: 		{
855: 			name:          "runtime without an existence check keeps creating",
856: 			checkable:     false,
857: 			failCreateNet: false,
858: 			wantExists:    false,
859: 			wantCreateNet: true,
860: 		},
861: 	} {
862: 		t.Run(tc.name, func(t *testing.T) {
863: 			setMgmtResolveBehavior(t, &mgmtResolveBehavior{
864: 				checkable:     tc.checkable,
865: 				networkExists: tc.networkExists,
866: 				failCreateNet: tc.failCreateNet,
867: 			})
868: 
869: 			c, err := NewContainerLab(
870: 				WithTimeout(time.Minute),
871: 				WithTopoPath(topo, nil),

900: // TestMakeCopyForDestroyIgnoresMgmtNetLinkChecks verifies that a lab can be destroyed
901: // after its topology dropped a management network a mgmt-net link still selects.
902: func TestMakeCopyForDestroyIgnoresMgmtNetLinkChecks(t *testing.T) {
903: 	registerMgmtResolveRuntime()
904: 	setMgmtResolveBehavior(t, &mgmtResolveBehavior{checkable: true, failCreateNet: true})
905: 
906: 	topo := writeTopo(t, `
907: name: mgmtnet-dropped
908: mgmt:
909:   - network: mgmtnet-dropped-a
910: topology:
911:   nodes:
912:     n1:
913:       kind: linux
914:       image: alpine
915:   links:
916:     - type: mgmt-net
917:       network: mgmtnet-dropped-b
918:       endpoint:
919:         node: n1
920:         interface: eth1
921:       host-interface: n1-eth1
922: `)
923:

925: 		t.Fatal("topology with an undefined mgmt-net link network was accepted")
926: 	}
927: 
928: 	c, err := NewContainerLab(
929: 		WithTimeout(time.Minute),
930: 		WithSkippedMgmtNetLinksCheck(),
931: 		WithTopoPath(topo, nil),
932: 		WithRuntime("mgmtresolvetest", &clabruntime.RuntimeConfig{}),
933: 		WithSkippedBindsPathsCheck(),
934: 	)
935: 	if err != nil {
936: 		t.Fatal(err)
937: 	}
938: 
939: 	cc, err := c.makeCopyForDestroy(context.Background(), topo, "", *NewDestroyOptions())
940: 	if err != nil {
941: 		t.Fatalf("makeCopyForDestroy failed: %v", err)
942: 	}
943: 	// the mgmt-net link nodes are shared by the package, release their endpoints
944: 	t.Cleanup(cc.resetResolvedLinks)
945: }
