The behavior is read by every instance NewContainerLab creates for\n// the duration of one test.\ntype destroyTestBehavior struct {\n\t// containers is returned by the first ListContainers call.\n\tcontainers []clabruntime.GenericContainer\n\t// failCreateNet makes CreateNet return an error, which fails makeCopyForDestroy.\n\tfailCreateNet bool\n\t// listErrAfterFirst makes every ListContainers call after the first return an\n\t// error, which fails the inner destroy after the copy was built.\n\tlistErrAfterFirst bool\n\t// listEmptyAfterFirst makes every ListContainers call after the first return no\n\t// containers, so the inner destroy completes without touching nodes.\n\tlistEmptyAfterFirst bool\n}\n\nvar destroyTestRuntimeBehavior atomic.Pointer[destroyTestBehavior]\n\n// destroyTestListCalls counts ListContainers calls across every fake runtime instance\n// the destroy tests create: the first call is the outer Destroy inventory, the later\n// ones belong to the CLab copy makeCopyForDestroy builds.\nvar destroyTestListCalls atomic.Int32\n\nvar registerDestroyTestRuntimeOnce sync.Once\n\n// registerDestroyTestRuntime registers a minimal fake container runtime the Destroy\n// unit tests can drive without a container engine.\nfunc registerDestroyTestRuntime() {\n\tregisterDestroyTestRuntimeOnce.Do(func() {\n\t\tclabruntime.Register(\"destroytest\", func() clabruntime.ContainerRuntime {\n\t\t\treturn &fakeDestroyRuntime{}\n\t\t})\n\t})\n}\n\n// setDestroyTestBehavior installs the behavior for one test and resets the call\n// bookkeeping the previous test left behind.\nfunc setDestroyTestBehavior(t *testing.T, behavior *destroyTestBehavior) {\n\tt.Helper()\n\n\tdestroyTestListCalls.Store(0)\n\tdestroyTestRuntimeBehavior.Store(behavior)\n\n\tt.Cleanup(func() {\n\t\tdestroyTestRuntimeBehavior.Store(nil)\n\t})\n}\n\ntype fakeDestroyRuntime struct {\n\tclabruntime.ContainerRuntime\n\tmgmt   *clabtypes.MgmtNet\n\tconfig *clabruntime.RuntimeConfig\n}\n\nfunc (f *fakeDestroyRuntime) Init(opts ...clabruntime.RuntimeOption) error {\n\tfor _, opt := range opts {\n\t\topt(f)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (f *fakeDestroyRuntime) WithConfig(cfg *clabruntime.RuntimeConfig) { f.config = cfg }\n\nfunc (f *fakeDestroyRuntime) WithMgmtNet(mgmt *clabtypes.MgmtNet) { f.mgmt = mgmt }\n\nfunc (f *fakeDestroyRuntime) Mgmt() *clabtypes.MgmtNet { return f.mgmt }\n\nfunc (*fakeDestroyRuntime) GetName() string { return \"destroytest\" }\n\nfunc (f *fakeDestroyRuntime) Config() clabruntime.RuntimeConfig {\n\tif f.config == nil {\n\t\treturn clabruntime.RuntimeConfig{}\n\t}\n\n\treturn *f.config\n}\n\nfunc (*fakeDestroyRuntime) GetRuntimeSocket() (string, error) { return \"\", nil }\n\nfunc (f *fakeDestroyRuntime) ListContainers(\n\t_ context.Context,\n\t_ []*clabtypes.GenericFilter,\n) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {\n\tbehavior := destroyTestRuntimeBehavior.Load()\n\tif behavior == nil {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\tif destroyTestListCalls.Add(1) > 1 {\n\t\tswitch {\n\t\tcase behavior.listErrAfterFirst:\n\t\t\treturn nil, errors.New(\"synthetic runtime failure\")\n\t\tcase behavior.listEmptyAfterFirst:\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn behavior.containers, nil\n}\n\nfunc (*fakeDestroyRuntime) errors.New(\"synthetic network creation failure\")\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (*fakeDestroyRuntime) DeleteNet(context.Context) error { return nil }\n\nfunc (*fakeDestroyRuntime) DeleteContainer(context.Context, string) error { return nil }\n\nfunc (*fakeDestroyRuntime) GetContainerStatus(\n\tcontext.Context,\n\tstring,\n) clabruntime.ContainerStatus {\n\treturn Reasoning
No test for the new behavior (containers==0 → special link nodes deleted, or wg.Wait ordering). The Ponytail rule says non-trivial logic leaves one runnable check. The new early path is small; a test would be nice but the change is tiny. I could note as P4/P3 coverage gap: no test covers the new early-return deletion path or ordering. Actually a test for the ordering race is hard (needs netlink). The early path could be testable with the fake runtime (listEmptyAfterFirst exists! TestDestroyCleanupRemovesLabDirsOnSuccess uses listEmptyAfterFirst with a topology with no links though — c.Links would be empty so deleteSpecialLinkNodes not invoked). A test with a host link in the topology and empty containers would exercise it (would need host link node Delete with no endpoints — no-op since endpoints are only attached if link resolves and isInFilter... Actually resolution DOES attach the endpoint to the host node even in the test env — AddEndpoint is called during Resolve. Delete then iterates endpoints → l.Remove → netlink ops that fail in test env (no privileges) → log.Warn. Observable? Hard. But could be observed via behavior differences. Hmm, this is a minor coverage note.

Now, let me carefully consider a possible correctness issue in the new early path: `destroy()` early return when containers==0. In this path, `deleteSpecialLinkNodes` is called — but NOT `CleanupFilteredLinks`. Is that a problem? `CleanupFilteredLinks` handles links of filtered-out nodes. Let me look at what it does.

Reasoning
OK, so the vxlan-stitch link's veth component attaches an endpoint to the host link node (via `lhr.Resolve(params)` → `hostEp.GetNode().AddEndpoint(hostEp)`). The host node singleton holds the veth host-side endpoint whose link is the `LinkVEth` (not the stitched). Hmm — wait: `hl` is a `LinkVEth` (from LinkHostRaw.Resolve) but the VxlanStitched wraps it. When node workers delete container endpoints, `e.GetLink().Remove(ctx)` — for the container endpoint of the stitched veth component, the link is the `LinkVEth` (vethLink) — wait, but the container endpoint's GetLink would be the LinkVEth, whose Remove deletes both endpoints (container side + host side veth). Meanwhile the special host node Delete → `l.Remove` where l is... the host endpoint's link. What link does the host node's endpoint for the vxlan-stitch have? The stitchEp from the veth component: `endpointByInterfaceName(eps, hostIface)` from `hl.GetEndpoints()` — the LinkVEth's endpoints. So ep.GetLink() returns the LinkVEth. So the special host node Delete calls vethLink.Remove (deletes both veth ends).

And the VXLAN part: who deletes the VXLAN interface? `VxlanStitched.Remove` calls `l.vxlanLink.Remove`. Where is VxlanStitched.Remove triggered during destroy? It must be via c.Links teardown somewhere — maybe `deleteLinks`? Let me search for where links get removed wholesale at destroy... Actually maybe node Delete for the container endpoint uses `e.GetLink()` which for the vxlan endpoint is the VxlanStitched? Let's check `resolveLocalEndpoint(stitched=true, ...)` — the local endpoint may belong to the host node and its link set to the stitched link. Let me check.
